{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}

In order to prove Green’s formulas, we need to define traces of functions in
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
on the transverse face
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
defined by (
3.4
) for
\tau\in\overline{I_{x}}
. Throughout the section, we assume for simplicity that
\tau=0\in\overline{I_{x}}
, so that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
. The extension to an arbitrary
\tau\in\overline{I_{x}}
is explained in Remark
3.9
.

Note that

\forall\;V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}),\quad V,\;\partial_{x}V\in L^{2}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}).

Therefore, the usual trace theorem applied to each
1
D function
{x}\mapsto V({x},{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\in H^{1}(I_{x})
provides an estimate which can be integrated with respect to
({z}_{1},{z}_{2})\in I_{1}\times(0,1)
, to obtain the following preliminary result.

Proposition 3.7
.

The trace application
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
, defined by
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}V:=V|_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
for
V\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\overline{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})
, extends by continuity to a linear map still denoted by
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
, and defined from
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
.

In what follows, we will abusively write
V|_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
instead of
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}V
when referring to traces on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
.

We are next interested in characterizing the range of the trace map
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
. To this end, we first extend the definition of
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
to functions defined on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
. For any
\Phi\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\overline{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})
such that
\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\Phi\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}})
, we can define
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\Phi=\Phi_{\mathbb{\Theta}}
as in the volume case (
3.13
) by choosing
({\bm{x}},s)\in\widehat{I}\times\mathbb{R}
, where
\widehat{I}=\widehat{I}^{\tau=0}
is the edge in (
3.8
). From

\displaystyle\forall\;{\bm{x}}=(0,{z})\in\widehat{I},\quad\forall\;s\in\mathbb{R},\qquad{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\Phi({\bm{x}},s):=\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\Phi(0,\;\theta_{1}\mskip 1.5mu{z},\;\theta_{2}\mskip 1.5mu{z}+s),

we deduce that
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\Phi(\cdot,s)
is simply the trace of
\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\Phi
along the line
\{(\theta_{1}\mskip 1.5mu{z},\;\theta_{2}\mskip 1.5mu{z}+s),\ {z}\in\mathbb{R}\}
. Similarly to Proposition
3.2
,
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
extends by density as an isomorphism from
L^{2}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}(0,1;L^{2}(\widehat{I}))
.

Consider the space

\boxed{H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}):=\big\{\Phi\in L^{2}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})\ /\ {\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\Phi\in L^{2}(0,1;H^{1/2}(\widehat{I}))\big\},}

(3.19)

which is equipped with the norm
\Phi\mapsto\|{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\Phi\|_{L^{2}(0,1;H^{1/2}(\widehat{I}))}
. Then the next result holds.

L^{2}(0,1;H^{1}(Q))
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
L^{2}(0,1;H^{1/2}(\widehat{I}))
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
\gamma_{0}
L^{2}(0,1;H(\mathop{{\hbox{\rm div}}}\nolimits;Q))
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
L^{2}(0,1;H^{-1/2}(\widehat{I}))
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
\gamma_{1,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
\gamma_{1}

Figure 9
:
Left: In Proposition
3.8
, the range of
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
is characterized using the shear map
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
and the usual trace operator
\gamma_{0}
defined by (
3.21
). More precisely,
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}={\mathscr{S}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}}\,\gamma_{0}{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
. Right: the same holds for the normal trace operator
\gamma_{1,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
defined in Proposition
3.11
for
I_{1}=\mathbb{R}
.

Proposition 3.8
.

The trace operator
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
is continuous and surjective from
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
, and commutes with the shear map
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
:

\forall\;V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}),\quad\forall\;s\in(0,1),\quad{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}(V|_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})(\cdot,s)={\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V(\cdot,s)|_{\widehat{I}}.

(3.20)

Proof
.

Equation (
3.20
) is obtained easily for smooth functions
V\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\overline{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})
using the definition of
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
. It extends to
V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
using:
(i)
the density of
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\overline{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})
in
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
,
(ii)
the continuity of
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
from
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}(0,1;H^{1}(Q))
(Corollary
3.4
) and from
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}(0,1;H^{1/2}(\widehat{I}))
(by definition (
3.19
)), and
(iii)
the continuity from
L^{2}(0,1;H^{1}(Q))
to
L^{2}(0,1;H^{1/2}(\widehat{I}))
of the trace map
\gamma_{0}
given by:

\displaystyle\forall\;V_{\mathbb{\Theta}}\in L^{2}(0,1;H^{1}(Q)),\quad\gamma_{0}V_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\cdot,s):=V_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\cdot,s)|_{\widehat{I}},

(3.21)

which is a direct consequence of the classical trace theorem in
H^{1}(Q)
.

Since
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
is an isomorphism, (
3.20
) gives
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}={\mathscr{S}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}}\,\gamma_{0}{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
(see Figure
9
left). The continuity and surjectivity of
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
then result from the continuity of
(\gamma_{0},{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu},{\mathscr{S}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}})
and the surjectivity of
\gamma_{0}
.

Remark 3.9
.

Let
\tau\in\overline{I}_{x}
. By applying the above arguments to
V(\cdot+\tau\,\textnormal{{e}}_{x})
where
V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
, one can define the trace of
V
on the face
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
for any
\tau\in\overline{I_{x}}
.

3.4
Normal trace operator and Green’s formula for a strip

In this section, we restrict ourselves to the case (

⁢
C
.1

) of a strip: more precisely, define

\displaystyle\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}:=I_{x}\times\mathbb{R}\times(0,1)\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}:=\{\tau\}\times\mathbb{R}\times(0,1).

Note that the strip
\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
and the face
\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
correspond respectively to the domains
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
defined by (
3.4
), with

I_{1}=\mathbb{R}.

Moreover, the domain
Q
and the edge
\widehat{I}^{\tau}
defined by (
3.8
) become

Q=I_{x}\times\mathbb{R}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\widehat{I}^{\tau}=\{\tau\}\times\mathbb{R}.

In what follows, we assume that
I_{x}\neq\mathbb{R}
, so that
\partial I_{x}\neq\varnothing
; typically
I_{x}=\mathbb{R}_{+}
,
\mathbb{R}_{-}
, or
(a,b)
. We fix
\tau\in\partial I_{x}
. Let
\textnormal{{n}}=\pm\textnormal{{e}}_{x}
(
resp.

{\bm{n}}=\pm{\bm{e}}_{x}
) denote the unit normal vector on
\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
(
resp.

\widehat{I}^{\tau}
) which is outward with respect to
\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
(
resp.

Q
). Our objective is to define a normal trace operator on
\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
and to prove the Green’s formula given in Proposition
3.11
.

The topological dual space of
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
is denoted by
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
, and is equipped with the dual norm for now. Let
\langle\cdot,\,\cdot\rangle_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
denote the duality product between
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
and
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
, defined as a natural extension of the
\vphantom{H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})}L^{2}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
–scalar product. In order to define a normal trace operator, we first extend the shear map
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
as an isomorphism from
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
[L^{2}(0,1;H^{1/2}(\widehat{I}^{\tau}))]^{\prime}
. This is achieved through duality, using the Parseval-like formula (
3.14
):

\displaystyle\forall\;\Psi\in H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}),\qquad\frac{1}{\theta_{1}}\big\langle{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\Psi,\Phi_{\mathbb{\Theta}}\big\rangle_{[L^{2}(0,1;H^{1/2}(\widehat{I}^{\tau}))]^{\prime},L^{2}(0,1;H^{1/2}(\widehat{I}^{\tau}))}\\
\quad:=\big\langle\Psi,{\mathscr{S}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}}\Phi_{\mathbb{\Theta}}\big\rangle_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}},\qquad\forall\;\Phi_{\mathbb{\Theta}}\in L^{2}(0,1;H^{1/2}(\widehat{I}^{\tau})).

(3.22)

The next lemma provides a more convenient characterization of
L^{2}(0,1;H^{1/2}(\widehat{I}^{\tau}))^{\prime}
.

Lemma 3.10
.

Given
a<b\in\mathbb{R}
and a Hilbert space
(\mathscr{X},(\cdot,\cdot)_{\mathscr{X}})
, one has
[L^{2}(a,b;\mathscr{X})]^{\prime}=L^{2}(a,b;\mathscr{X}^{\prime})
as well as the following identity: for any
\varPhi\in L^{2}(a,b;\mathscr{X})
and
\varPsi\in L^{2}(a,b;\mathscr{X}^{\prime})
:

\displaystyle\big\langle\varPsi,\varPhi\big\rangle_{L^{2}(a,b;\mathscr{X})^{\prime},L^{2}(a,b;\mathscr{X})}=\int_{a}^{b}\big\langle\varPsi(\cdot,s),\;\varPhi(\cdot,s)\big\rangle_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathscr{X}}\;ds,

(3.23)

where
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathscr{X}}
denotes the duality product between
\mathscr{X}^{\prime}
and
\mathscr{X}
.

Proof
.

This result holds for a more general class of Banach spaces (see e.g.
[
9
]
), but we provide a simpler proof for Hilbert spaces, for ease of the reader. One checks without any difficulty that the inclusion
[L^{2}(a,b;\mathscr{X})]^{\prime}\supset L^{2}(a,b;\mathscr{X}^{\prime})
holds. Conversely, the inclusion
[L^{2}(a,b;\mathscr{X})]^{\prime}\subset L^{2}(a,b;\mathscr{X}^{\prime})
and (
3.23
) result from the Riesz representation theorem applied to
\mathscr{X}
and to the space
L^{2}(a,b;\mathscr{X})
equipped with the scalar product

\displaystyle(\varPhi,\varPsi)\mapsto\int_{a}^{b}(\varPhi(\cdot,s),\varPsi(\cdot,s))_{\mathscr{X}}\;ds.

By applying Lemma
3.10
with
(a,b)=(0,1)
and
\mathscr{X}:=H^{1/2}(\widehat{I}^{\tau})
, we deduce that
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
is an isomorphism from
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}(0,1;H^{-1/2}(\widehat{I}^{\tau}))
. Moreover, the bijectivity of
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
implies that:

\boxed{H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})=\big\{\Psi\ /\ {\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\Psi\in L^{2}(0,1;H^{-1/2}(\widehat{I}^{\tau}))\big\}.}

(3.24)

Finally, (
3.22
) combined with (
3.23
) leads to the following, which is analogous to (
3.14
):

\displaystyle\forall\;(\Phi,\Psi)\in H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})\times H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}),\quad\big\langle\Psi,\;\Phi\big\rangle_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}=\frac{1}{\theta_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\big\langle{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\Psi(\cdot,s),\;{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\Phi(\cdot,s)\big\rangle_{\widehat{I}^{\tau}}\;ds.

(3.25)

In what follows,
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
is endowed with the norm
\Psi\mapsto\|{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\Psi\|_{L^{2}(0,1;H^{-1/2}(\widehat{I}^{\tau}))}
.

At last, we are able to define the normal trace operator thanks to the next result.

Proposition 3.11
.

Given
\tau\in\partial I_{x}
, the normal trace application
\gamma^{\tau}_{1,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
defined by
\gamma^{\tau}_{1,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\bm{W}:=({\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}\cdot\textnormal{{n}})|_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
for any
\bm{W}\in[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})]^{2}
, extends by continuity to a linear and surjective map still denoted by
\gamma^{\tau}_{1,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
, and defined from
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
.

Moreover, we have the Green’s formula: for any
V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
and
\bm{W}\in H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\big[(\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut\bm{\nabla}}{\mathbb{\Theta}}\bm{W})\;\overline{V}+\bm{W}\cdot\overline{\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}V}\big]=\sum_{\tau\in\partial I_{x}}\big\langle{\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}\cdot\textnormal{{n}},\;V\big\rangle_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}},

(3.26)

where we have written abusively
{\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}\cdot\textnormal{{n}}\equiv({\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}\cdot\textnormal{{n}})|_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
instead of
\gamma^{\tau}_{1,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\bm{W}
.

Remark 3.12
.

Note that the integrals on
\partial\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}\cap\{{z}_{2}\in\{0,1\}\}
do not appear in the Green’s formula (
3.26
) because the functions are
“periodic”
in the
\textnormal{{e}}_{2}
–direction (see Remark
3.6
).

Proof
(of Proposition
3.11
).

Similarly to the proof of Proposition
3.8
, we begin by observing that
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
commutes with
\gamma^{\tau}_{1,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
in the sense that for any
\bm{W}\in[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})]^{2}
(with
\textnormal{{n}}=\pm\textnormal{{e}}_{x}
and
{\bm{n}}=\pm{\bm{e}}_{x}
),

\displaystyle\forall\;s\in(0,1),\quad{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\big(({\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}\cdot\textnormal{{n}})|_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\big)(\cdot,s)=\big({\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\bm{W}(\cdot,s)\cdot{\bm{n}}\big)|_{\widehat{I}^{\tau}}.

(3.27)

Equation (
3.27
) extends to
\bm{W}\!\in\!H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
using
(i)
the density of
[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})]^{2}
in
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
,
(ii)
the continuity of the shear map
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
from
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}(0,1;H(\mathop{{\hbox{\rm div}}}\nolimits;Q))
(Corollary
3.4
) and from
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to the space
L^{2}(0,1;H^{-1/2}(\widehat{I}^{\tau}))
(by definition (
3.19
)), and
(iii)
the continuity from
L^{2}(0,1;H(\mathop{{\hbox{\rm div}}}\nolimits;Q))
to
L^{2}(0,1;H^{-1/2}(\widehat{I}^{\tau}))
of the following normal trace application:

\displaystyle\forall\;\bm{W}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}\in L^{2}(0,1;H(\mathop{{\hbox{\rm div}}}\nolimits;Q)),\quad\gamma_{1}\bm{W}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\cdot,s):=\big(\bm{W}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\cdot,s)\cdot{\bm{n}}\big)|_{\widehat{I}^{\tau}},

which follows directly from the classical trace theorem in
H(\mathop{{\hbox{\rm div}}}\nolimits;Q)
.

Since
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
is an isomorphism, (
3.20
) implies that
\gamma^{\tau}_{1,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}={\mathscr{S}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}}\,\gamma_{1}{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
(see Figure
9
right). The continuity and surjectivity of
\gamma^{\tau}_{1,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
then follows from the continuity of
(\gamma_{1},{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu},{\mathscr{S}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}})
and from the surjectivity of
\gamma_{1}
.

To finish we prove the Green’s formula (
3.26
). For
V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
and
\bm{W}\in H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
, the classical Green formula applied to
({\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V(\cdot,s),{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\bm{W}(\cdot,s))\in H^{1}(Q)\times H(\mathop{{\hbox{\rm div}}}\nolimits;Q)
for almost any
s\in(0,1)
and integrated with respect to
s
leads to

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{Q}\big[\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut\nabla}_{\bm{x}}({\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\bm{W})\;\overline{{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V}+{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\bm{W}\cdot\overline{\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V}]({\bm{x}},s)\;d{\bm{x}}ds\\
=\sum_{\tau\in\partial I_{x}}\int_{0}^{1}\big\langle({\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\bm{W}(\cdot,s)\cdot{\bm{n}})|_{\widehat{I}^{\tau}},\;{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V(\cdot,s)|_{\widehat{I}^{\tau}}\big\rangle_{\widehat{I}^{\tau}}\;ds,

where we recall that
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\widehat{I}^{\tau}}
is the duality product between
H^{-1/2}(\widehat{I}^{\tau})
and
H^{1/2}(\widehat{I}^{\tau})
. To conclude, we use the properties (
3.14
,
3.17
,
3.18
) of
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
for the left-hand side, as well as the identity (
3.25
) for the right-hand side.

3.5
Subspaces of periodic functions in a cylinder

We now adress the case (

⁢
C
.2

) of the cylinder. In addition to the strip
\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}=I_{x}\times\mathbb{R}\times(0,1)
and the interface
\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}=\{\tau\}\times\mathbb{R}\times(0,1)
defined in Section
3.4
, we also introduce the cylinder and the interface

\displaystyle\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}:=I_{x}\times(0,1)\times(0,1)\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}:=\{\tau\}\times(0,1)\times(0,1),

(3.28)

where the subscript
“

\textstyle{\#}^{2}

”
refers to the boundedness of the domains in the
\textnormal{{e}}_{1}
and the
\textnormal{{e}}_{2}
–directions.

Note that
\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
and
\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
correspond respectively to the domains
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{O}}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbb{\Gamma}}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
in (
3.4
) with

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}I_{1}=(0,1).}

Therefore, one can use the spaces
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
,
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
defined by (
3.16
), as well as the space
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
defined by (
3.19
). In the sequel, we assume that
I_{x}\neq\mathbb{R}
, so that
\partial I_{x}\neq\varnothing
.

For the purpose of Sections
4.2
and
4.3
, we want to define spaces of
H^{1}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}
or
H_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\mathbf{div})
–functions in
\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
which are
1
–periodic with respect to both
{z}_{1}
and
{z}_{2}
. To do so, let us begin with the following definition.

Definition 3.13
.

Let
\bm{V}\in[L^{2}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})]^{d}
. The
periodic extension
\mathrm{E}^{1}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\bm{V}\in[L^{2}_{\textit{loc}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})]^{d}
of
\bm{V}
in the
\textnormal{{e}}_{1}
–direction
is defined by:

\operatorname{a.e.}\;\textnormal{{x}}=({x},{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\in\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}},\quad\forall\;n\in\mathbb{Z},\quad(\mathrm{E}^{1}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\bm{V})(\textnormal{{x}}+n\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1}):=\bm{V}(\textnormal{{x}}).

(3.29)

For
\Phi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
, we define
\mathrm{E}^{1}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\Phi\in L^{2}_{\textit{loc}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
similarly, with
\textnormal{{x}}\in\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
instead of
\textnormal{{x}}\in\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
.

In addition, let
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}})
be the space of smooth functions in
\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}
that are compactly supported in the
\textnormal{{e}}_{x}
–direction and
1
–periodic in the
\textnormal{{e}}_{1}
and
\textnormal{{e}}_{2}
–directions, namely

\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}):=\big\{V\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}})\ /\ \mathrm{E}^{1}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5muV\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})\big\},

(3.30)

where
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})
is given by (
3.12
). Note that
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}})
contains
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
, and therefore, is dense in
L^{2}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
.

Now, we introduce
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
(
resp.

H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
), which is defined as the closure of
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}})
(
resp.

[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}})]^{2}
) in
H^{1}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
(
resp.

H_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
):

\displaystyle\boxed{H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}):=\overline{\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}})}^{{H^{1}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})}}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}):=\overline{[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}})]^{2}}^{{H_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})}}.}

(3.31)

We note that
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
and
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
are respectively closed subspaces of
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
and
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
, and hence, are Hilbert spaces when equipped with the respective norms of these last spaces.

Remark 3.14
.

Similarly to Remark
3.6
, for smooth functions, the definition of
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
corresponds to periodicity in the
\textnormal{{e}}_{1}
and
\textnormal{{e}}_{2}
–directions. More generally, one could show the characterization

H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})=\{V\in H^{1}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})\ /\ V|_{{z}_{1}=0}=V|_{{z}_{1}=1}\ \textnormal{and}\ V|_{{z}_{2}=0}=V|_{{z}_{2}=1}\},

where the equalities have to be understood not in the
L^{2}
sense, but in the
L^{2}_{\textit{loc}}
sense. We refer to
[
2
, Section 3.2.1]
for similar considerations.

Section
3.3
and Proposition
3.8
applied for
I_{1}=(0,1)
allow to define the trace operator on
\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
(
\tau\in\partial I_{x}
) for functions in
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
. Introduce

\displaystyle H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}):=\big\{V|_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}\ /\ \ V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})\big\},

(3.32)

to which we associate the graph norm. Let
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
be the topological dual space of
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
, equipped with the dual norm. The duality product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}
between
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
and
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
is defined as a natural extension of the
\smash{L^{2}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})}
–scalar product.

The density of
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}})
in
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
, the density of
[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}})]^{2}
in
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
, and the continuity of the trace operator on
\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
lead directly to the next result.

Proposition 3.15
.

For
\tau\in\partial I_{x}
, the normal trace map
\gamma^{\tau}_{1,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}
defined by
\gamma^{\tau}_{1,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}\bm{W}:=({\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}\cdot\textnormal{{n}})|_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}
for any
\smash{\bm{W}\in[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}})]^{2}}
extends by continuity to a linear and surjective map still denoted by
\gamma^{\tau}_{1,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}
, and defined from
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
to
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
.

Moreover, we have the Green’s formula: for any
V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
and
\bm{W}\in H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}\big[(\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut\bm{\nabla}}{\mathbb{\Theta}}\bm{W})\;\overline{V}+\bm{W}\cdot\overline{\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}V}\big]=\sum_{\tau\in\partial I_{x}}\big\langle({\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}\cdot\textnormal{{n}}),\;V\big\rangle_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}},

(3.33)

where we have abusively written
({\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}\cdot\textnormal{{n}})\equiv({\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}\cdot\textnormal{{n}})|_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}
instead of
\gamma^{\tau}_{1,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}\bm{W}
.

We finish with the following result, which will be used in Section
4.3
. The proof is similar to the proof of the jump rule for isotropic Sobolev spaces, and relies on the Green’s formula (
3.33
).

Proposition 3.16
.

Assume that
\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}=\mathbb{\Omega}^{1}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}\cup\mathbb{\Omega}^{2}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
where
\mathbb{\Omega}^{1}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
and
\mathbb{\Omega}^{2}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
are disjoint cylinders defined by

\mathbb{\Omega}^{\ell}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}:=J^{\ell}_{x}\times(0,1)\times(0,1)\subset\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}},\quad\forall\;\ell\in\{1,2\}.

where
J^{1}_{x}\cup J^{2}_{x}=I_{x}
and
\overline{J^{1}_{x}}\cap\overline{J^{2}_{x}}=\{\tau\}
(see Figure
10
). Let
\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}:=\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}^{1}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}\cap\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}^{2}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}
.

(\textit{a})
.

Let
V_{\ell}\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}^{\ell}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
,
\ell\in\{1,2\}
. Then the function defined by

\displaystyle\operatorname{a.e.}\;\textnormal{{x}}\in\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}},\quad V(\textnormal{{x}}):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}V_{1}(\textnormal{{x}})&\textnormal{if}\quad\textnormal{{x}}\in\mathbb{\Omega}^{1}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}\\[4.0pt]
V_{2}(\textnormal{{x}})&\textnormal{if}\quad\textnormal{{x}}\in\mathbb{\Omega}^{2}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}\end{array}\right.

belongs to
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
if and only if
V_{1}|_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}=V_{2}|_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}
.

(\textit{b})
.

Let
\bm{W}_{\ell}\in H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}^{\ell}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
,
\ell\in\{1,2\}
. Then the function given by

\displaystyle\operatorname{a.e.}\;\textnormal{{x}}\in\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}},\quad\bm{W}(\textnormal{{x}}):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\bm{W}_{1}(\textnormal{{x}})&\textnormal{if}\quad\textnormal{{x}}\in\mathbb{\Omega}^{1}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}\\[4.0pt]
\bm{W}_{2}(\textnormal{{x}})&\textnormal{if}\quad\textnormal{{x}}\in\mathbb{\Omega}^{2}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}\end{array}\right.

belongs to
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
if and only if
({\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}_{1}\cdot\textnormal{{e}}_{x})|_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}=({\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}_{2}\cdot\textnormal{{e}}_{x})|_{\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}
.

{x}
{z}_{1}
\mathbb{\Omega}^{2}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
\mathbb{\Omega}^{1}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
\smash{{z}_{2}}

Figure 10
:
The domains
\mathbb{\Omega}^{1}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
and
\mathbb{\Omega}^{2}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
in Proposition
3.16
for

,

, and
