.

4
The solution of the augmented periodic problem

Using the function spaces introduced in Section
3
, we propose in this section a rigorous formulation of the augmented problem (
3.1
). This problem is then studied and solved by exploiting the periodic nature of
(\mathbb{A}_{p},\rho_{p})
, following the steps (
a
)–(
c
) described in Section
1.2
.

4.1
The augmented strip problem and its quasi-2D structure

We consider the
3
–dimensional strips
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}},\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\pm}
and the 2–dimensional interface
\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
defined by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{r@{\ :=\ }l}\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}&\{({x},{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\ /\ {z}_{2}\in(0,1)\},\\[10.0pt]
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\pm}&\{({x},{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\in\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}\ /\ \pm{x}>0\},\\[10.0pt]
\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}&\{({x},{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\in\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}\ /\ {x}=0\}.\end{array}

(4.1)

These domains are represented in Figure
11
. We shall use the indexing
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\nu}
,
{\nu}\in\{\varnothing,+,-\}
where by convention,
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\nu}=\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
for
{\nu}=\varnothing
. Note that
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\nu}
and
\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
correspond respectively to the domains
\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}:=I_{x}\times\mathbb{R}\times(0,1)
and
\mathbb{\Sigma}^{\tau}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}:=\{\tau\}\times\mathbb{R}\times(0,1)
introduced in Section
3.4
, with

I_{x}:=\mathbb{R}_{\nu},\quad Q=\mathbb{R}_{\nu}\times\mathbb{R},\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\widehat{I}=\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}.

Therefore, Sections
3.2
,
3.3
, and
3.4
enable us to use the spaces
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\nu})
,
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\nu})
defined by (
3.16
), the space
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
given by (
3.19
) and its dual
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
characterized by (
3.24
), and the trace and normal trace applications on
\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
.

{x}
{z}_{1}
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{-}
\smash{{z}_{2}}
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{+}

Figure 11
:
The half-strips
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{+}
and
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{-}
defined in (
4.1
)

Using the formal observations in the step (
a
) of Section
1.2
, we introduce for any
G\in H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
the strip problem:
find
U\in H^{1}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
such that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\ =\ }l}\displaystyle-\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut\bm{\nabla}}\,{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu\mathbb{A}_{p}\!\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}\,U-\rho_{p}\,\omega^{2}\,U&0\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{+}\cup\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{-},\\[12.0pt]
\lx@intercol\hfil\displaystyle U\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}},{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}),\ \ (\mathbb{A}_{p}\!\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}\,U)|_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\pm}}\in H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\pm}),\hfil\lx@intercol\\[12.0pt]
\displaystyle\llbracket({\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu\mathbb{A}_{p}\!\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}\,U)\cdot\textnormal{{e}}_{x}\rrbracket_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}&G,\end{array}\right.

(
\mathscr{P}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
)

where
\mathbb{A}_{p},\rho_{p}
are given by (
2.5
) for Configuration (

A

) and by (
2.10
,
2.12
) for Configuration (

B

). Note that from the first equation, one has
\smash{(\mathbb{A}_{p}\!\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}\,U)|_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\pm}}\in H_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\mathbf{div};\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\pm})}
. Formally speaking, the second equation requires
U
and
\smash{(\mathbb{A}_{p}\!\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}\,U)|_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\pm}}}
to be
1
–periodic with respect to
{z}_{2}
(see Remark
3.6
). Finally,
\smash{\llbracket{\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}\cdot\textnormal{{e}}_{x}\rrbracket_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}}
is the jump accross
\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
of the normal components of a function
\bm{W}\in[L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})]^{2}
which satisfies
\bm{W}^{\pm}:=\bm{W}|_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\pm}}\in H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\pm})
:

\llbracket{\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}\cdot\textnormal{{e}}_{x}\rrbracket_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}:=({\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}^{-}\cdot\textnormal{{e}}_{x})|_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}-({\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W}^{+}\cdot\textnormal{{e}}_{x})|_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}},

(4.2)

and the third equation holds in
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
.

In what follows, we shall study Problem (

P
#

) and emphasize its fibered structure as a
“concatenation”
of
2
–dimensional problems. For that latter purpose, it is useful to introduce the family of functions indexed by
s\in\mathbb{R}
:

\displaystyle\forall\;s\in\mathbb{R},\quad\mathbb{A}_{s}({\bm{x}}):=\mathbb{A}_{p}({\mathbb{\Theta}}\,{\bm{x}}+s\,\textnormal{{e}}_{2})\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\rho_{s}({\bm{x}}):=\rho_{p}({\mathbb{\Theta}}\,{\bm{x}}+s\,\textnormal{{e}}_{2}),\quad\forall\;{\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

(4.3)

Note that the restrictions of
\mathbb{A}_{s}
and
\rho_{s}
to
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\pm}
are well-defined and continuous since for both Configurations (

A

) and (

B

),
\mathbb{A}_{p}
and
\rho_{p}
depend in a simple manner on
\mathbb{A}
and
\rho
which are continuous on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\pm}
. Furthermore,
s\mapsto\mathbb{A}_{s}
and
s\mapsto\rho_{s}
are continuous from
\mathbb{R}
to
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2})
and
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
respectively. Note also that for any
s\in\mathbb{R}
,
\mathbb{A}_{s}
and
\rho_{s}
satisfy the ellipticity assumption (
1.2
), and that
\mathbb{A}_{s+1}=\mathbb{A}_{s}
and
\rho_{s+1}=\rho_{s}
.

Given
\psi\in H^{-1/2}(\sigma)
, we consider the
2
–dimensional problem:
Find
u_{s}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
such that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\ =\ }l}\displaystyle-\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut\nabla}\mathbb{A}_{s}({\bm{x}})\,\nabla u_{s}({\bm{x}})-\rho_{s}({\bm{x}})\,\omega^{2}\,u_{s}({\bm{x}})&0\quad\textnormal{for}\ \ {\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}\cup\mathbb{R}^{2}_{-},\\[8.0pt]
\displaystyle\llbracket\mathbb{A}_{s}\,\nabla u_{s}\cdot{\bm{e}}_{x}\rrbracket_{\sigma}&\psi.\end{array}\right.

(
\mathscr{P}_{s}
)

Remark 4.1
.

One has
\mathbb{A}_{0}=\mathbb{A}
and
\rho_{0}=\rho
, and (

P

) corresponds to (

P
s

) with
s=0
and
\psi=g
.

Because of the ellipticity assumption (
1.2
) and the presence of absorption (
1.1
), Problem (

P
s

) admits a unique solution
u_{s}[\psi]\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Furthermore, the well-posedness of Problem (

P
#

) and the link between its solution
U
and the solutions
u_{s}[\psi]
is given by the following result.

Proposition 4.2
.

For any
G\in H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
, Problem (

P
#

) is equivalent to the variational problem

\left|\begin{array}[]{l}\textit{Find $U\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})$ such that $\forall\;V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})$,}\\[12.0pt]
\lx@intercol\hfil\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\big[\,(\mathbb{A}_{p}\!\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}\,U)\cdot\overline{\vphantom{U^{+}}(\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}\,V)}-\rho_{p}\,\omega^{2}\,U\,\overline{V}\,\big]=\big\langle G,\;V\big\rangle_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\hfil\lx@intercol,\end{array}\right.

(
\mathrm{FV}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
)

which is well-posed in
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
. Furthermore,

\operatorname{a.e.}\;s\in(0,1),\quad\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5muU({x},\theta_{1}\,{z},\theta_{2}\,{z}+s)=u_{s}({\bm{x}}),\quad\operatorname{a.e.}\;{\bm{x}}=({x},{z})\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

(4.4)

where
U=U[G]
and
u_{s}=u_{s}[g_{s}]
with
g_{s}:={\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}{G}(\cdot,s)
are the respective solutions of Problems (

P
#

) and (

P
s

).

Proof
.

To obtain the variational formulation (

FV
#

), one multiplies the volume equation in (

P
#

) by
V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
, and integrates over
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{+}
and
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{-}
separately. Since
\mathbb{A}_{p}\!\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}\,U\in H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\pm})
, we then apply Green’s formula (
3.26
) on each domain
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{+}
and
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{-}
, add the corresponding identities, and we finally use the transmission condition (that is, the third equation in (

P
#

)) to conclude.

The well-posedness of the variational formulation (

FV
#

) is guaranteed by Lax-Milgram’s theorem. In particular, the bilinear form
\mathscr{A}
associated to (

FV
#

) satisfies

\forall\;V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}),\quad\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits\Big[\frac{\mathscr{A}(V,V)}{\omega}\Big]=-\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits\omega\;\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\Big[\,\frac{1}{|\omega|^{2}}\,\mathbb{A}_{p}|\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}\,V|^{2}+\rho_{p}\,|V|^{2}\,\Big],

(4.5)

and therefore is coercive in
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
due to the presence of absorption (
1.1
), that is,
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits\omega>0
.

Now we shall prove (
4.4
), which will enable us to deduce the equivalence between (

FV
#

) and (

P
#

) at last. To this end, given
s\in(0,1)
, we show that
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}U(\cdot,s)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
satisfies the same problem as
u_{s}[{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}G(\cdot,s)]
. For
V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
, the properties (
3.17
,
3.14
) of
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
combined with (

FV
#

) and (
3.25
) imply that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big[\,\mathbb{A}_{s}\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}U(\cdot,s)\cdot\overline{\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V(\cdot,s)}

\displaystyle-\rho_{s}\,\omega^{2}\,{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}U(\cdot,s)\,\overline{{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V(\cdot,s)}\,\big]d{\bm{x}}ds

\displaystyle=\theta_{1}\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\big[\,(\mathbb{A}_{p}\!\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}\,U)\cdot\overline{\vphantom{U^{+}}(\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}\,V)}-\rho_{p}\,\omega^{2}\,U\,\overline{V}\,\big]

\displaystyle=\theta_{1}\,\big\langle G,\;V\big\rangle_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}=\int_{0}^{1}\big\langle{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}G(\cdot,s),\;{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V(\cdot,s)|_{\sigma}\big\rangle_{\sigma}\;ds.

(4.6)

Now we choose
V
such that
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V({\bm{x}},s)=\varphi(s)\,v({\bm{x}})
, with
\varphi\in L^{2}(0,1)
and
v\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
. Then it follows that
\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V({\bm{x}},s)=\varphi(s)\,\nabla v({\bm{x}})
, and (
4.6
) being true for any
\varphi\in L^{2}(0,1)
leads for almost any
s\in(0,1)
to the equation

\forall\;v\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}),\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big[\,\mathbb{A}_{s}\nabla_{\bm{x}}{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}U(\cdot,s)\cdot\overline{\nabla v}-\rho_{s}\,\omega^{2}\,{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}U(\cdot,s)\,\overline{v}\,\big]\;d{\bm{x}}=\big\langle{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}G(\cdot,s),\;v\big\rangle_{\sigma},

which is equivalent to the transmission problem (

P
s

) satisfied by
u_{s}[{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}G(\cdot,s)]
. From the uniqueness of the solution of (

P
s

), we then deduce
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}U(\cdot,s)=u_{s}[{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}G(\cdot,s)]
which is (
4.4
).

It remains to prove the equivalence between Problem (

P
#

) and its variational formulation (

FV
#

). But we have shown in the last step that if
U
satisfies (

FV
#

), then
({\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}U)(\cdot,s)=u_{s}[{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}G(\cdot,s)]
. Combining this result with the properties (
3.17
,
3.18
) of
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
thus leads to the first and second equations in (

P
#

), while the transmission condition (that is, the third equation in (

P
#

)) follows from (
3.25
) and from the transmission condition satisfied by
u_{s}[{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}G(\cdot,s)]
in (

P
s

).

To conclude this section, we propose in Proposition
4.4
a rigorous formulation of the ansatz (
1.8
). To begin, note that due to the uniform boundedness of
(\mathbb{A}_{s})_{s}
and
(\rho_{s})_{s}
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
,
(u_{s}[\psi])_{s}
is bounded uniformly with respect to
s
:

\displaystyle\exists\;c>0,\quad\forall\;s\in\mathbb{R},\quad\big\|u_{s}[\psi]\big\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\leq c\;\|\psi\|_{H^{-1/2}(\sigma)},\quad\forall\;\psi\in H^{-1/2}(\sigma).

(4.7)

By combining this property with the continuity of
\mathbb{A}_{s}
and
\rho_{s}
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with respect to
s
, one can show that
s\mapsto u_{s}[\psi]
is a continuous application. This is the object of the next proposition, whose proof is similar to the proof of
[
2
, Proposition 3.18]
.

Proposition 4.3
.

For
\psi\in H^{-1/2}(\sigma)
,
s\mapsto u_{s}[\psi]
is continuous from
\mathbb{R}
to
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
and is
1
–periodic.

The next proposition shows how the solution
u
of (

P

) can be retrieved from the solution
U[G]
of the augmented problem (

P
#

) for a well-chosen boundary data
G
(see Remark
4.5
).

Proposition 4.4
.

Consider
G\in H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
such that
s\mapsto{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}G(\cdot,s)\in H^{-1/2}(\sigma)
is continuous at
0
, and that
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}G(\cdot,0)=g
, where
g\in H^{-1/2}(\sigma)
is the jump data in (

P

). Then setting
U=U[G]
, the map
s\mapsto({\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}U)(\cdot,s)\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
is continuous as well at
0
, and

\displaystyle\operatorname{a.e.}\;{\bm{x}}=({x},{z})\in\mathbb{R}^{2},\quad u({\bm{x}})=\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5muU({x},\theta_{1}\,{z},\theta_{2}\,{z}).

(4.8)

Proof
.

Since
\psi\mapsto u_{s}[\psi]
is continuous from
H^{-1/2}(\sigma)
to
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
uniformly with respect to
s
(according to (
4.7
)) on one hand, and
s\mapsto u_{s}[\psi]
is continuous (according to Proposition
4.3
) on the other hand, it follows that
s\mapsto u_{s}[{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}G(\cdot,s)]
is continuous at
0
as a product of continuous maps. Consequently (
4.4
) becomes true for
s=0
, and thus leads to (
4.8
).

Remark 4.5
.

If
g\in L^{2}(\sigma)
, a class of augmented data
G\in H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
such that
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}G(\cdot,0)=g
is given by

\operatorname{a.e.}\;(0,{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\in\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}},\quad G(0,{z}_{1},{z}_{2}):=f({z}_{2}-{z}_{1}\theta_{2}/\theta_{1})\,g(0,{z}_{1}/\theta_{1}),

where
f\in\mathscr{C}^{0}(\mathbb{R})
is such that
f(s+1)=f(s)
and
f(0)=1
(e.g.
f(s)=\mathrm{e}^{2\mathrm{i}\pi\ell s}
,
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
). In fact, it can be seen that
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}G(0,{z},0):=\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5muG(0,\theta_{1}{z},\theta_{2}{z})=f(0)g(0,{z})=g(0,{z})
. If
g\in H^{-1/2}(\sigma)
, then the above expression of
G
can be extended by duality.

4.2
Reduction to waveguide problems via the Floquet-Bloch transform

Now that we have shown that to solve the original problem (

P

), it is sufficient to study the augmented strip problem (

P
#

), we begin by taking advantage of the periodicity with respect to
{z}_{1}
by applying a Floquet-Bloch transform. Using the properties of the
1
D Floquet-Bloch transform (see
[
29
]
or
[
31
, Section 2.2]
), we introduce in Section
4.2.1
the partial Floquet-Bloch transform with respect to
{z}_{1}
, and present its properties in
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
or
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
. The transform is then applied to the solution of (

P
#

) in Section
4.2.2
, leading to a family of half-guide problems denoted (

P

#
2

) later on, and parameterized by the Floquet dual variable.

4.2.1
The partial Floquet-Bloch transform with respect to
{z}_{1}

We begin by introducing

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{l@{\quad}r@{\ :=\ \{(\xvi, \zvi_1, \zvi_2) \in\ }l@{\ /\ \zvi_1 \in(0, 1) \},}}\forall\;{\nu}\in\{\varnothing,+,-\},&\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{\nu}\ :=\ \{({x},{z}_{1},{z}_{2}&\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}^{\nu}\hfil\ /\ {z}_{1}\\[10.0pt]
&\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}\ :=\ \{({x},{z}_{1},{z}_{2}&\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}\hfil\ /\ {z}_{1}\end{array}

(4.9)

where the subscript
“

\textstyle{\#}^{2}

”
indicates the boundedness of the domains in the
\textnormal{{e}}_{1}
and the
\textnormal{{e}}_{2}
–directions. These domains are represented in Figure
12
. Note that
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{\nu}
and
\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
correspond respectively to the domains
\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}:=I_{x}\times(0,1)\times(0,1)
and
\mathbb{\Sigma}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}:=\{0\}\times(0,1)\times(0,1)
defined by (
3.28
) with

I_{x}:=\mathbb{R}_{\nu},\quad Q=\mathbb{R}_{\nu}\times(0,1/\theta_{1}),\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\widehat{I}=\{0\}\times(0,1/\theta_{1}).

Therefore, thanks to Section
3.5
, we can use the spaces
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
,
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
given by (
3.31
) (see also Remark
3.14
for the meaning of these spaces), as well as the space
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
defined by (
3.32
), its dual
\smash{H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})}
, and the trace and normal trace operators on
\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
.

{x}
{z}_{1}
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{-}
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{+}
\smash{{z}_{2}}

Figure 12
:
The half-cylinders
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{+}
and
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{-}
defined in (
4.9
)

Let
d\in\{1,2\}
. The Floquet-Bloch transform in the
\textnormal{{e}}_{1}
–direction is defined by

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{r@{\ }c@{\ }c@{\ }l}{\mathcal{F}}:&\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\overline{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})^{d}&\longrightarrow&[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}\times\mathbb{R})]^{d}\\[5.0pt]
&\bm{V}&\longmapsto&\widehat{\bm{V}},\end{array}\qquad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle\widehat{\bm{V}}(\textnormal{{x}},{k}):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\bm{V}(\textnormal{{x}}+n\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu({z}_{1}+n)},\\[16.0pt]
\quad\forall\;\textnormal{{x}}=({x},{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\in\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}},\ {k}\in\mathbb{R}.\end{array}\right.

(4.10)

Note that
\textnormal{{x}}\mapsto\widehat{\bm{V}}(\textnormal{{x}},{k})
is
\mathbb{Z}\textnormal{{e}}_{1}
–periodic, and
{k}\mapsto\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu{z}_{1}}\,\widehat{\bm{V}}(\textnormal{{x}},{k})
is
2\pi
–periodic. Therefore,
\widehat{\bm{V}}
can be fully constructed from its knowledge on the cell
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}\times(-\pi,\pi)
. For this reason, the study of
\widehat{\bm{V}}
will be restricted to
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}\times(-\pi,\pi)
.

The transform
{\mathcal{F}}
extends to an isometry from
[L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})]^{d}
to
L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;[L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})]^{d})
, with

\displaystyle\forall\;\bm{U},\bm{V}\in[L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})]^{d},\quad\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}{\mathcal{F}}\bm{U}(\textnormal{{x}},{k})\cdot\overline{{\mathcal{F}}\bm{V}(\textnormal{{x}},{k})}\;d\textnormal{{x}}d{k}=\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\bm{U}\cdot\overline{\bm{V}}.

(4.11)

Moreover,
{\mathcal{F}}
is an isomorphism, with the inversion formula: for any
\widehat{\bm{U}}\in L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;[L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})]^{d})
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{a.e.}\;\textnormal{{x}}=({x},{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\in\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}},\quad\forall\;n\in\mathbb{Z},\quad({\mathcal{F}^{-1}}\widehat{\bm{U}})(\textnormal{{x}}+n\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\widehat{\bm{U}}(\textnormal{{x}},{k})\;\mathrm{e}^{\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu({z}_{1}+n)}\;d{k}.

(4.12)

In addition, for
\Phi\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\overline{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})
, we define
\widehat{\Phi}:={\mathcal{F}}\Phi\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}\times\mathbb{R})
as in (
4.10
) by choosing
\textnormal{{x}}\in\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
. Then
{\mathcal{F}}
extends to an isometry between
L^{2}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
and
L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;L^{2}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))
.

What makes the Floquet-Bloch transform a valuable tool in the context of this paper is on one hand the fact that it commutes with the multiplication by
\mathbb{Z}\textnormal{{e}}_{1}
–periodic coefficients:

\displaystyle\forall\;\mu\in L^{\infty}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})\ \textnormal{such that}\ \mu(\cdot+\textnormal{{e}}_{1})=\mu,\quad\forall\;V\in L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}(\mu V)=\mu{\mathcal{F}}V,

(4.13)

and on the other hand its action on differential operators. More precisely, the next proposition gives the properties of
{\mathcal{F}}
in
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
,
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
, and
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
. Its proof is delayed to Appendix
B
.

Proposition 4.6
.

(\textit{a})
.

{\mathcal{F}}
is an isomorphism from
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))
, and

\displaystyle\forall\;V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}),\quad\operatorname{a.e.}\;{k}\in(-\pi,\pi),\quad{\mathcal{F}}(\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,\bm{\nabla}V)\mskip 1.5mu(\cdot,{k})=\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,{\mathcal{F}}V(\cdot,{k}).

(4.14)

(\textit{b})
.

{\mathcal{F}}
is an isomorphism from
H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))
, and

\displaystyle\forall\;\bm{W}\in H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}),\quad\forall\;{k}\in(-\pi,\pi),\quad{\mathcal{F}}(\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut\bm{\nabla}}{\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\bm{W})\mskip 1.5mu(\cdot,{k})=\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})}\,{\mathbb{\Theta}}\,{\mathcal{F}}\bm{W}(\cdot,{k}).

(4.15)

(\textit{c})
.

{\mathcal{F}}
is an isomorphism from
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))
.

We highlight the following consequence of Proposition
4.6
for future reference.

Corollary 4.7
.

For any
U,V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
,

\displaystyle\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,{\mathcal{F}}U(\textnormal{{x}},{k})\cdot\overline{\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,{\mathcal{F}}V(\textnormal{{x}},{k})}\;d\textnormal{{x}}d{k}=\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,\bm{\nabla}U\cdot\overline{\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,\bm{\nabla}V}.

(4.16)

Finally,
{\mathcal{F}}
extends by duality as an isomorphism from
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
\mathscr{X}^{\prime}:=[L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))]^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\forall\;\Psi\in H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}),\quad\big\langle{\mathcal{F}}\Psi,\;\widehat{\Phi}\big\rangle_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\mathscr{X}}:=\big\langle\Psi,\;{\mathcal{F}^{-1}}\widehat{\Phi}\big\rangle_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}},\quad\forall\;\widehat{\Phi}\in\mathscr{X}.

Since
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
is a Hilbert space, Lemma
3.10
implies
\mathscr{X}^{\prime}=L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))
and

\displaystyle\forall\;(\Phi,\Psi)\in H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})\times\smash{H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})},\quad\big\langle\Psi,\;\Phi\big\rangle_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\big\langle{\mathcal{F}}\Psi(\cdot,{k}),\;{\mathcal{F}}\Phi(\cdot,{k})\big\rangle_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}\;d{k}.

(4.17)

4.2.2
Application to the augmented strip problem

Thanks to the properties of the Floquet-Bloch transform
{\mathcal{F}}
given in Section
4.2.1
, we deduce directly the following proposition.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
G\in H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
. Then the solution
U[G]\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
of (

P
#

) is given by

\displaystyle\operatorname{a.e.}\;\textnormal{{x}}=({x},{z}_{1},{z}_{2})\in\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}},\quad\forall\;n\in\mathbb{Z},\quad U[G](\textnormal{{x}}+n\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\,\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\widehat{U}_{k}[\widehat{G}_{k}]\mskip 1.5mu(\textnormal{{x}})\;\mathrm{e}^{-\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu({z}_{1}+n)}\;d{k},

(4.18)

where
\widehat{G}_{k}:={\mathcal{F}}G(\cdot,{k})\in H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})

\operatorname{a.e.}\;{k}\in(-\pi,\pi)
, and where
\widehat{U}_{k}[\widehat{G}_{k}]:={\mathcal{F}}U[G]\mskip 1.5mu(\cdot,{k})
is the unique solution of the well-posed waveguide problem: find
\widehat{U}_{k}\in H^{1}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
such that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\ =\ }l}\displaystyle-\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})}\,{\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\mathbb{A}_{p}\!\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,\widehat{U}_{k}-\rho_{p}\,\omega^{2}\,\widehat{U}_{k}&0\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{+}\cup\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{-},\\[12.0pt]
\lx@intercol\hfil\displaystyle\widehat{U}_{k}\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}),\ \ (\mathbb{A}_{p}\!\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,\widehat{U}_{k})|_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{\pm}}\in H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{\pm}),\hfil\lx@intercol\\[12.0pt]
\displaystyle\llbracket({\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu\mathbb{A}_{p}\!\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,\widehat{U}_{k})\cdot\textnormal{{e}}_{x}\rrbracket_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}&\widehat{G}_{k},\end{array}\right.

(
\mathscr{P}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
)

whose variational formulation is given by

\left|\begin{array}[]{l}\textit{Find $\widehat{U}_{k}\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})$ such that $\forall\;V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})$,}\\[10.0pt]
\lx@intercol\hfil\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}\big[\,(\mathbb{A}_{p}\mskip 1.5mu\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,\widehat{U}_{k}\cdot\overline{\vphantom{U^{+}}\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,V}-\rho_{p}\,\omega^{2}\,\widehat{U}_{k}\,\overline{V}\,\big]=\big\langle\widehat{G}_{k},\;V\big\rangle_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}.\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}\right.

(
\mathrm{FV}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
)

Proof
.

On one hand, Proposition
4.6
applied to (

P
#

) shows that
\widehat{U}_{k}[\widehat{G}_{k}]:={\mathcal{F}}U[G]\mskip 1.5mu(\cdot,{k})
satisfies (

P

#
2

). On the other hand, Corollary
4.7
applied to the variational formulation (

FV
#

) implies that
\widehat{U}_{k}
satisfies (

FV

#
2

). The equivalence between (

P

#
2

) and (

FV

#
2

) then follows from the equivalence between the strip problem (

P
#

) and its variational formulation (

FV
#

) (Proposition
4.2
).

Proposition
4.8
shows that
U[G]
can be reconstructed in the strip
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
, provided that one knows how to solve the waveguide problem (

P

#
2

) for any
{k}\in(-\pi,\pi)
. The solution of this waveguide problem is the object of the next section.

4.3
Reducing the waveguide problem to the interface
\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}

In this section, the Floquet variable
{k}\in(-\pi,\pi)
is fixed. We investigate the waveguide problem (

P

#
2

), whose solution is denoted by
\widehat{U}_{k}[\widehat{G}_{k}]
. To this end, we reformulate the problem as an equation on
\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
involving DtN operators associated to half-guide problems defined in
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{\pm}
.

Given boundary data
\Phi\in H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
, we consider the half-guide problem:
find
\widehat{U}^{\pm}_{k}\in H^{1}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
such that

\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\ =\ }l}\displaystyle-\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})}\,{\mathbb{\Theta}}\,\mathbb{A}^{\pm}_{p}\!\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,\widehat{U}^{\pm}_{k}-\rho^{\pm}_{p}\,\omega^{2}\,\widehat{U}^{\pm}_{k}&0\quad\textnormal{in}\ \ \Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{\pm},\\[12.0pt]
\lx@intercol\hfil\displaystyle\widehat{U}^{\pm}_{k}\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{\pm}),\ \ \mathbb{A}^{\pm}_{p}\!\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,\widehat{U}^{\pm}_{k}\in H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{\pm}),\hfil\lx@intercol\\[12.0pt]
\displaystyle\widehat{U}^{\pm}_{k}&\Phi\quad\textnormal{on}\ \ \Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}.\end{array}\right.

(
\mathscr{P}^{\pm}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
)

Under Assumptions (
1.1
,

), Lax-Milgram’s theorem combined with a lifting argument ensures that (

P
±

#
2

) admits a unique solution

\widehat{U}^{\pm}_{k}[\Phi]\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{\pm}).

Let
\widehat{\rule{0.0pt}{5.38193pt}\smash{\Lambda}}\vphantom{\Lambda}^{\pm}_{k}\in\mathscr{L}(H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}),H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))
be the DtN operator defined by

\displaystyle\forall\;\Phi\in H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}),\quad\widehat{\rule{0.0pt}{5.38193pt}\smash{\Lambda}}\vphantom{\Lambda}^{\pm}_{k}\,\Phi:=\big({\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu\mathbb{A}_{p}\!\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\mskip 1.5mu\,\widehat{U}^{\pm}_{k}[\Phi]\cdot\textnormal{{n}}\big)|_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}},

or equivalently (thanks to the Green’s formula (
3.33
))

\displaystyle\forall\;\Phi,\Psi\in H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}),\quad\big\langle\widehat{\rule{0.0pt}{5.38193pt}\smash{\Lambda}}\vphantom{\Lambda}^{\pm}_{k}\,\Phi,\;\Psi\big\rangle_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}=\mathscr{A}^{\pm}_{k}\big(\widehat{U}^{\pm}_{k}[\Phi],\,\widehat{U}^{\pm}_{k}[\Psi]\big),\\[8.0pt]
\textnormal{where}\quad\mathscr{A}^{\pm}_{k}(U,V):=\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{\pm}}\big[\,(\mathbb{A}^{\pm}_{p}\mskip 1.5mu\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,U\cdot\overline{\vphantom{U^{+}}\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,V}-\rho^{\pm}_{p}\,\omega^{2}\,U\,\overline{V}\,\big].

(4.19)

The above weak form allows to show the following result.

Proposition 4.9
.

The operators
\widehat{\rule{0.0pt}{5.38193pt}\smash{\Lambda}}\vphantom{\Lambda}^{+}_{k}
,
\widehat{\rule{0.0pt}{5.38193pt}\smash{\Lambda}}\vphantom{\Lambda}^{-}_{k}
, and
\widehat{\rule{0.0pt}{5.38193pt}\smash{\Lambda}}\vphantom{\Lambda}^{+}_{k}+\widehat{\rule{0.0pt}{5.38193pt}\smash{\Lambda}}\vphantom{\Lambda}^{-}_{k}
are coercive and thus invertible from
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
to
H^{-1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
.

Proof
.

Let
{\nu}\in\{+,-\}
. From (
4.19
), we have for any
\Phi\in H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
the equality

\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits\bigg[\frac{\big\langle\widehat{\rule{0.0pt}{5.38193pt}\smash{\Lambda}}\vphantom{\Lambda}^{\nu}_{k}\,\Phi,\;\Phi\big\rangle_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}}{\omega}\bigg]=-\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits\omega\;\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\bigg[\,\frac{1}{|\omega|^{2}}\,\mathbb{A}_{p}\big|\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,\widehat{U}^{\nu}_{k}[\Phi]\big|^{2}+\rho_{p}\,|\widehat{U}^{\nu}_{k}[\Phi]|^{2}\,\bigg],

implying that
\widehat{\rule{0.0pt}{5.38193pt}\smash{\Lambda}}\vphantom{\Lambda}^{\nu}_{k}
is coercive, due to absorption (
\mathop{\mathfrak{Im}}\nolimits\omega>0
) and to the continuity of the trace operator on
\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
. The same holds for
\widehat{\rule{0.0pt}{5.38193pt}\smash{\Lambda}}\vphantom{\Lambda}^{+}_{k}+\widehat{\rule{0.0pt}{5.38193pt}\smash{\Lambda}}\vphantom{\Lambda}^{-}_{k}
by summing the above equality over
{\nu}\in\{+,-\}
.

By linearity and by uniqueness of the solutions of (

P

#
2

) and (

P
±

#
2

), the waveguide solution
\widehat{U}_{k}[\widehat{G}_{k}]
admits the expression

\displaystyle\operatorname{a.e.}\;\textnormal{{x}}\in\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}},\quad\widehat{U}_{k}[\widehat{G}_{k}](\textnormal{{x}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\widehat{U}^{+}_{k}[\Phi_{k}]\mskip 1.5mu(\textnormal{{x}})&\textnormal{if}\quad\textnormal{{x}}\in\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{+},\\[4.0pt]
\widehat{U}^{-}_{k}[\Phi_{k}]\mskip 1.5mu(\textnormal{{x}})&\textnormal{if}\quad\textnormal{{x}}\in\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{-},\end{array}\right.

(4.20)

where
\Phi_{k}:=\widehat{U}_{k}|_{\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}

4.4
The DtN approach for the half-guide problems

Our objective is to solve the half-guide problems (

±

#
2

) defined in
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{+}
and

\widehat{\rule{0.0pt}{5.38193pt}\smash{\Lambda}}\vphantom{\Lambda}^{+}_{k}
and
\widehat{\rule{0.0pt}{5.38193pt}\smash{\Lambda}}\vphantom{\Lambda}^{-}_{k}
. Since these problems are similar to each other, we will restrict ourselves to the half-guide problem (
\mathscr{P}^{+}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}

, and the computation of
\smash{\widehat{\rule{0.0pt}{5.38193pt}\smash{\Lambda}}\vphantom{\Lambda}^{+}_{k}}

\mathbb{A}^{+}_{p}
and
\rho^{+}_{p}
in the
\textnormal{{e}}_{x}
–direction, we resort to the method developed in

23
]
for the elliptic Helmholtz equation

, and in
[
2
]
for the non-elliptic
2

We also introduce some additional notation:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{0}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}:=(0,1)^{3}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\mathcal{C}^{n}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}:=\mathcal{C}^{0}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}+n\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{x}\quad\forall\;n\in\mathbb{N},\quad\textnormal{so that}\quad\overline{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}^{+}}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\overline{\mathcal{C}^{n}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}.
