7.2
Short-term perspective on junctions of general periodic half-spaces

We recall that the transmission configurations studied throughout the paper are characterized by a pair
(\mathbb{A},\rho)
, where
\mathbb{A}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}
(
resp.

\rho:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
) coincides in
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\pm}
with a function
\mathbb{A}^{\pm}
(
resp.

\rho^{\pm}
) with periodicity vectors
{\bm{p}}^{\pm}_{1},{\bm{p}}^{\pm}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, as illustrated in Figure
1
(see also Figure
30
top). Configuration (

A

) and Configuration (

B

) were particular cases for which the associated transmission problem (

P

) could be lifted into a
3
D augmented problem.

In this section, we describe how one can also derive an augmented formulation for arbitrary vectors
{\bm{p}}^{\pm}_{1}
and
{\bm{p}}^{\pm}_{2}
. This will be the focus of Sections
7.2.2
and
7.2.3
. We will rely on a notion of equivalence between two transmission configurations, which is introduced in Section
7.2.1
.

7.2.1
A notion of equivalence between two transmission problems

In this section, we work with pairs
(\mathbb{T}_{+},\mathbb{T}_{-})
of matrices such that

\textnormal{$\mathbb{T}_{\pm}\in\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}$ is invertible},\quad\mathbb{T}_{\pm}\,\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\pm}=\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\pm}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\mathbb{T}_{+}\,{\bm{e}}_{z}=\mathbb{T}_{-}\,{\bm{e}}_{z}={\bm{e}}_{z}.

(7.1)

From each pair, we can construct the piecewise-defined map
\mathbb{T}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
:

\displaystyle\forall\;{\bm{x}}=({x},{z})\in\mathbb{R}^{2},\quad\mathbb{T}({\bm{x}}):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l@{\quad\textnormal{if}\ \ }l}\mathbb{T}_{+}\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{x}}&x>0\\[5.0pt]
\mathbb{T}_{-}\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{x}}&x<0.\end{array}\right.

(7.2)

Note that by (
7.1
),
\mathbb{T}
is a continuous bijection from
\mathbb{R}^{2}
to
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, which is piecewise differentiable, with Jacobian matrix

\displaystyle\forall\;{\bm{x}}=({x},{z})\in\mathbb{R}^{2},\quad\mathbb{J}_{\mathbb{T}}({\bm{x}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l@{\quad\textnormal{if}\ \ }l}\mathbb{T}_{+}&x>0\\[5.0pt]
\mathbb{T}_{-}&x<0,\end{array}\right.

so that it defines a continuous and piecewise smooth change of variables
{\bm{x}}\mapsto\mathbb{T}{\bm{x}}
. We shall say that two transmission configurations associated to pairs
(\mathbb{A},\rho)
and
(\widetilde{\mathbb{A}},\widetilde{\rho})
are
equivalent
if and only if there exists a transformation
\mathbb{T}
defined by (
7.1
,
7.2
), such that

\forall\;{\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2},\qquad\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}({\bm{x}})=\mathbb{J}_{\mathbb{T}}({\bm{x}})\;\mathbb{A}\big(\mathbb{T}^{-1}({\bm{x}})\big)\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut[\mathbb{J}_{\mathbb{T}}({\bm{x}})]}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\widetilde{\rho}({\bm{x}})=\rho\big(\mathbb{T}^{-1}({\bm{x}})\big).

(7.3)

The first interest of the notion of equivalence introduced above is that the solutions
u
and
\widetilde{u}
of the transmission problems (

P

) associated to
(\mathbb{A},\rho)
and
(\widetilde{\mathbb{A}},\widetilde{\rho})
, respectively, are simply related through the change of variables
{\bm{x}}\mapsto\mathbb{T}{\bm{x}}
. This is the object of the next proposition, which is easily proved using the chain rule.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
u
and
\widetilde{u}
be the respective solutions of the transmission problems (

P

) associated to the pairs
(\mathbb{A},\rho)
and
(\widetilde{\mathbb{A}},\widetilde{\rho})
related by (
7.3
). Then

\displaystyle\widetilde{u}({\bm{x}})=u\big(\mathbb{T}^{-1}({\bm{x}})\big).

(7.4)

{\bm{q}}^{+}_{1}
{\bm{q}}^{+}_{2}
{\bm{q}}_{1}^{-}
{\bm{q}}_{2}^{-}
x
z

{\bm{e}}_{x}
{\bm{p}}^{+}
{\bm{e}}_{x}
{\bm{p}}^{-}
{x}
{z}

Figure 30
:
Junction of arbitrary periodic half-spaces (top). Thanks to Propositions
7.1
and
7.2
, this configuration can always be reduced to a junction of periodic half-spaces with one periodicity direction being
{\bm{e}}_{x}
(bottom).

A useful property is that any transmission configuration
(\mathbb{A},\rho)
, with periodicity vectors
({\bm{p}}^{\pm}_{1},{\bm{p}}^{\pm}_{2})
, is equivalent, in the sense of (
7.3
), to a transmission configuration
(\widetilde{\mathbb{A}},\widetilde{\rho})
where one of the periodicity vectors in each half-space is
{\bm{e}}_{x}
(see in Figure
30
bottom).

Proposition 7.2
.

Given a transmission configuration
(\mathbb{A},\rho)
with periodicity vectors
({\bm{p}}^{\pm}_{1},{\bm{p}}^{\pm}_{2})
, there exists a pair
(\widetilde{\mathbb{A}},\widetilde{\rho})
equivalent to
(\mathbb{A},\rho)
in the sense of (
7.3
), such that
\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}
(
resp.

\widetilde{\rho}
) concides in
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\pm}
with a
\mathbb{Z}{\bm{e}}_{x}+\mathbb{Z}\widetilde{{\bm{p}}}^{\,\pm}
–periodic function
\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}^{\,\pm}
(
resp.

\widetilde{\rho}^{\,\pm}
), with
\widetilde{{\bm{p}}}^{\,\pm}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Proof
.

Consider the matrices
\mathbb{T}_{+},\mathbb{T}_{-}\in\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}
defined by

\mathbb{T}_{\pm}\,{\bm{e}}_{x}={\bm{p}}^{\pm}_{1}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\mathbb{T}_{\pm}\,{\bm{e}}_{z}={\bm{e}}_{z}.

(7.5)

Such matrices are invertible if and only if
\smash{{\bm{p}}^{\pm}_{1}=(p^{\pm}_{1,{x}},p^{\pm}_{1,{z}})}
satisfies
p^{\pm}_{1,{x}}\neq 0
. This assumption can be made without any loss of generality: in fact, since
\smash{{\bm{p}}^{\pm}_{1}}
and
\smash{{\bm{p}}^{\pm}_{2}}
are non-collinear, at least one of them is not collinear with
{\bm{e}}_{z}
. Moreover, because
(|p^{\pm}_{1,{x}}|,p^{\pm}_{1,{z}})
is also a periodicity vector of
(\mathbb{A}^{\pm},\rho^{\pm})
, we can assume that
p^{\pm}_{1,{x}}>0
, so that
\mathbb{T}_{\pm}\,\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\pm}=\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\pm}
. Finally, note that
\mathbb{T}_{+}\,{\bm{e}}_{z}=\mathbb{T}_{-}\,{\bm{e}}_{z}={\bm{e}}_{z}
, by definition. Consequently, the pair
(\mathbb{T}_{+},\mathbb{T}_{-})
satisfies the properties (
7.1
).

Finally, let
(\widetilde{\mathbb{A}},\widetilde{\rho})
be defined by (
7.3
). Then using (
7.5
), one easily checks that
\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}
(
resp.

\widetilde{\rho}
) concides in
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\pm}
with a
\mathbb{Z}{\bm{e}}_{x}+\mathbb{Z}\widetilde{{\bm{p}}}^{\,\pm}
–periodic function
\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}^{\,\pm}
(
resp.

\widetilde{\rho}^{\,\pm}
), with
\widetilde{{\bm{p}}}^{\,\pm}:=\mathbb{T}^{-1}_{\pm}\,{\bm{p}}^{\pm}_{2}
.

7.2.2
General configurations that are equivalent to Configurations (

A

) and (

B

)

Proposition
7.2
implies that any arbitrary junction of periodic half-spaces can be reduced to the case of Figure
30
bottom. In particular,

1.

Any junction of
\mathbb{Z}{\bm{q}}^{\pm}+\mathbb{Z}(p^{\pm}_{z}{\bm{e}}_{z})
–periodic half-spaces can be reduced to Configuration (

A

);

2.

Any junction of a homogeneous half-space and a
\mathbb{Z}{\bm{q}}+\mathbb{Z}{\bm{p}}
–periodic half-space can be reduced to Configuration (

B

).

Therefore, by Proposition
7.1
, the solution of (

P

) for a junction of
\mathbb{Z}{\bm{q}}^{\pm}+\mathbb{Z}(p^{\pm}_{z}{\bm{e}}_{z})
–periodic half-spaces (
resp.
the junction of a homogeneous half-space and a
\mathbb{Z}{\bm{q}}+\mathbb{Z}{\bm{p}}
–periodic half-space) can be obtained using the method developed in this paper for Configuration (

A

) (
resp.
(

B

)).

7.2.3
Other configurations

We now address the general case of a transmission configuration
(\mathbb{A},\rho)
with arbitrary periodicity vectors. By Proposition
7.2
, one can assume without any loss of generality that the restriction
\mathbb{A}^{\pm}
of
\mathbb{A}
to
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{\pm}
is
\mathbb{Z}{\bm{e}}_{x}+\mathbb{Z}{\bm{p}}^{\pm}
–periodic, with
{\bm{p}}^{\pm}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
(and similarly for
\rho
). By using the arguments developed in Section
2.2.2
for Configuration (

B

), we have the existence of
\theta^{\pm}_{1},\theta^{\pm}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathbb{Z}^{3}
–periodic functions
\mathbb{A}^{\pm}_{p}
such that

\displaystyle\forall\;{\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2},\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\ =\ }l}\mathbb{A}^{+}({x},{z})&\mathbb{A}^{+}_{p}({x},\theta^{+}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu{z},\theta^{+}_{2}\mskip 1.5mu{z})=\mathbb{A}^{+}_{p}({\mathbb{\Theta}}^{+}\,{\bm{x}}),\\[12.0pt]
\mathbb{A}^{-}({x},{z})&\mathbb{A}^{-}_{p}({x},\theta^{-}_{1}\mskip 1.5mu{z},\theta^{-}_{2}\mskip 1.5mu{z})=\mathbb{A}^{-}_{p}({\mathbb{\Theta}}^{-}\,{\bm{x}}),\end{array}\right.\qquad{\mathbb{\Theta}}^{\pm}:=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\theta^{\pm}_{1}\\
0&\theta^{\pm}_{2}\end{pmatrix}.

It is then natural to ask if one can lift the overall coefficient
\mathbb{A}
in a similar manner. This corresponds to finding
(i)
an integer
n\geq 2
,
(ii)
a
n
D coefficient
\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}_{p}
which coincides in
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\pm}
with a continuous function
\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}^{\pm}_{p}
which is
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
–periodic, and
(iii)
a matrix
\widetilde{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times 2}
, such that

\displaystyle\forall\;{\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2},\quad\mathbb{A}({\bm{x}})=\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}_{p}(\widetilde{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,{\bm{x}}).

Note that in general, one cannot choose
\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}^{\pm}_{p}=\mathbb{A}^{\pm}_{p}
, since the matrices
{\mathbb{\Theta}}^{+}
and
{\mathbb{\Theta}}^{-}
do not coincide. This is the reason why we need to introduce additional variables to construct
(n,\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}_{p},\widetilde{{\mathbb{\Theta}}})
.

1.

General case: reduction to an augmented
5
D problem
. One observes that in general,
\mathbb{A}
admits an extension
\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}_{p}
which is periodic, but defined in
\mathbb{R}^{5}
. In fact, it can be easily checked that
\mathbb{A}({\bm{x}})=\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}_{p}(\widetilde{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,{\bm{x}})
for any
{\bm{x}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, where

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}:=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\theta^{+}_{1}\\
0&\theta^{+}_{2}\\
0&\theta^{-}_{1}\\
0&\theta^{-}_{2}\end{pmatrix}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\ :=\ }l}\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}^{+}_{p}({x},{z}^{+}_{1},{z}^{+}_{2},{z}^{-}_{1},{z}^{-}_{2})&\mathbb{A}^{-}_{p}({x},{z}^{+}_{1},{z}^{+}_{2})\\[12.0pt]
\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}^{-}_{p}({x},{z}^{+}_{1},{z}^{+}_{2},{z}^{-}_{1},{z}^{-}_{2})&\mathbb{A}^{-}_{p}({x},{z}^{-}_{1},{z}^{-}_{2}).\end{array}\right.

The process for constructing
\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}
and
\widetilde{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}
will be presented in a subsequent paper.

2.

Further reduction to a
4
D augmented problem in the rationally dependent case
. We say that the family
\{\theta^{+}_{1},\theta^{+}_{2},\theta^{-}_{1},\theta^{-}_{2}\}
is
rationally dependent
if there exists a non-zero vector of integers
(m^{+}_{1},m^{+}_{2},m^{-}_{1},m^{-}_{2})\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\setminus\{0\}
such that
m^{+}_{1}\,\theta^{+}_{1}+m^{+}_{2}\,\theta^{+}_{2}+m^{-}_{1}\,\theta^{-}_{1}+m^{-}_{2}\,\theta^{-}_{2}=0
. In this case, if we assume that
m^{+}_{1}\neq 0
for simplicity, then one can avoid introducing an additional variable, for instance
{z}^{+}_{1}
, by observing that

\mathbb{A}({\bm{x}})=\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}_{p}(\widetilde{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,{\bm{x}})
with
n=4
,

\widetilde{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}:=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&\theta^{+}_{2}/m^{+}_{1}\\
0&\theta^{-}_{1}/m^{+}_{1}\\
0&\theta^{-}_{2}/m^{+}_{1}\end{pmatrix}\ \ \textnormal{and}\ \ \left\{\begin{array}[]{r@{\ :=\ }l}\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}^{+}_{p}({x},{z}^{+}_{2},{z}^{-}_{1},{z}^{-}_{2})&\mathbb{A}^{+}_{p}(x,-m^{+}_{2}\mskip 1.5mu{z}^{+}_{2}-m^{-}_{1}{z}^{-}_{1}-m^{-}_{2}{z}^{-}_{2},m^{+}_{1}{z}^{+}_{2})\\[12.0pt]
\widetilde{\mathbb{A}}^{-}_{p}({x},{z}^{+}_{2},{z}^{-}_{1},{z}^{-}_{2})&\mathbb{A}^{-}_{p}(x,m^{+}_{1}{z}^{-}_{1},m^{+}_{1}{z}^{-}_{2}).\end{array}\right.

Note that one could find an augmented structure with dimension
n<4
if there exists another non-trivial integer combination
\widehat{m}^{+}_{1}\,\theta^{+}_{1}+\widehat{m}^{+}_{2}\,\theta^{+}_{2}+\widehat{m}^{-}_{1}\,\theta^{-}_{1}+\widehat{m}^{-}_{2}\,\theta^{-}_{2}=0
. This would correspond to the transmission configurations in Section
7.2.2
.

If the family
\{\theta^{+}_{1},\theta^{+}_{2},\theta^{-}_{1},\theta^{-}_{2}\}
is
rationally independent
, that is
m^{+}_{1}\theta^{+}_{1}+m^{+}_{2}\theta^{+}_{2}+m^{-}_{1}\theta^{-}_{1}+m^{-}_{2}\theta^{-}_{2}\neq 0
for any
(m^{+}_{1},m^{+}_{2},m^{-}_{1},m^{-}_{2})\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\setminus\{0\}
, then we believe that one cannot find an augmented structure with dimension
n<5
, unless if
\mathbb{A}
is constant in one or multiple directions. This will be addressed in a future work.

7.3
The case without absorption

In the case where there is no absorption, the application of the method in this paper raises several challenges. First, some waves guided by the interface may exist (depending on the coefficients), so that the physical solution may propagate along the interface without attenuation. The use of the Floquet-Bloch transform along the 3D lifted interface is then not possible. If the solution is
L^{2}
along the interface (in the absence of these interface guided modes), the Floquet-Bloch transform can be applied and the numerical method can be formally extended (although one has to add conditions to select the outgoing waves, see
[
15
,
16
]
). A corresponding formal approach has already been used for the rational case
[
12
]
. However, even in the rational case, the rigorous justification of the numerical method is still a difficult challenge.

Appendix A
Anisotropic Sobolev spaces

A.1
Proof of Proposition
3.2
: properties of the shear map
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}

To prove Proposition
3.2
, we rely on the following easy but useful lemma, which links the integral of
\bm{V}
on
\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
and of
\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\bm{V}
on
\mathbb{\Omega}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}},\theta}
for
\bm{V}\in[L^{1}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})]^{d}
. We refer to
[
2
, Lemma 3.12]
for its proof.

Lemma A.1
.

For any
\bm{V}\in[L^{1}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})]^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\bm{V}=\int_{\mathbb{\Omega}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}},\theta}}\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\bm{V},

(A.1)

where
\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\bm{V}\in L^{1}_{\textit{loc}}(\mathbb{\Omega})^{d}
is defined by (
3.11
).

Proof
(of Proposition
3.2
).

The mapping
\operatorname{T}_{\theta}:({\bm{x}},s)\in Q\times(0,1)\mapsto{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\bm{x}}+s\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{2}\in\mathbb{\Omega}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}},\theta}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{1}
–diffeomorphism with a non-vanishing Jacobian determinant
\theta_{1}\neq 0
. Hence, the associated change of variables leads to

\displaystyle\forall\;\bm{U},\bm{V}\in[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})]^{d},\quad\frac{1}{\theta_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{Q}{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\bm{U}({\bm{x}},s)\cdot\overline{{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\bm{V}({\bm{x}},s)}\;d{\bm{x}}ds=\int_{\mathbb{\Omega}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}},\theta}}(\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\bm{U})\cdot(\overline{\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\bm{V}}).

We then use the identity
(\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\bm{U})\cdot(\overline{\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\bm{V}})=\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu(\bm{U}\cdot\overline{\bm{V}})
, and we apply Lemma
A.1
to
\bm{U}\cdot\overline{\bm{V}}
, to deduce (
3.14
) for
\bm{U},\bm{V}\in[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})]^{d}
. Finally, the density of
[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})]^{d}
in
[L^{2}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})]^{d}
leads to (
3.14
). Moreover, by choosing
\bm{U}=\bm{V}
in (
3.14
), it follows that
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
is bounded from
[L^{2}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})]^{d}
to
L^{2}(0,1;[L^{2}(Q)]^{d})
.

The bijectivity of
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
results directly from the inverse of
\operatorname{T}_{\theta}
, which leads to the expression (
3.15
) of
{\mathscr{S}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}}
. The continuity of
{\mathscr{S}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}}
is then implied by (
3.14
).

A.2
Proof of Proposition
3.3
: Action of the shear map
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
on differential operators

Proof
(of Proposition
3.3
).

We only prove (
3.17
) since the proof of (
3.18
) is very similar. The proof is merely a weak adaptation of the chain rule. Let
V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
. To obtain the expression of
\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}V
in the sense of distributions, consider a test function
\bm{W}\in[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})]^{2}
and define
\bm{W}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}:={\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}\bm{W}\in[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(Q\times(0,1))]^{2}
. Then
\mathrm{E}^{2}_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\mskip 1.5mu\bm{W}\in[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})]^{2}
since
\bm{W}\in[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})]^{2}
and one easily computes using the chain rule that

\displaystyle\forall\;({\bm{x}},s)\in Q\times(0,1),\quad{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}(\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut\bm{\nabla}}{\mathbb{\Theta}}\bm{W})({\bm{x}},s)=\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut\nabla}_{\bm{x}}\bm{W}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}({\bm{x}},s).

(A.2)

Let also
V_{\mathbb{\Theta}}:={\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V
. Using derivation in the sense of distributions with
V\in L^{2}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
leads to:

\displaystyle\big\langle\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}V,\;\bm{W}\big\rangle_{[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})^{2}]^{\prime},\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}V(\textnormal{{x}})\;\overline{\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut\bm{\nabla}}{\mathbb{\Theta}}\bm{W}(\textnormal{{x}})}\;d\textnormal{{x}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\theta_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{Q}{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V({\bm{x}},s)\ \overline{{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}(\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut\bm{\nabla}}{\mathbb{\Theta}}\bm{W})({\bm{x}},s)}\;d{\bm{x}}ds\quad\textnormal{from \eqref{H2Dmod:eq:oblique_plancherel}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\theta_{1}}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{Q}V_{\mathbb{\Theta}}({\bm{x}},s)\;\overline{\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut\nabla}_{\bm{x}}\bm{W}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}({\bm{x}},s)}\;d{\bm{x}}ds\quad\textnormal{from \eqref{H2Dmod:eq:link_H1Cper_H1C_and_HdivCper_HdivC:0}}.

(A.3)

But the definition of
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
implies that
V_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\cdot,s)\in H^{1}(Q)
,
\operatorname{a.e.}\;s\in(0,1)
. Thus in (
A.3
), we can apply the usual Green formula to
V_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\cdot,s)
and
\bm{W}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\cdot,s)\in[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(Q)]^{2}
(with no boundary term since
\bm{W}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\cdot,s)
is compactly supported) to obtain

\displaystyle\big\langle\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}V,\;\bm{W}\big\rangle_{[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})^{2}]^{\prime},\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})^{2}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{1}{\theta_{1}}}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{Q}\nabla_{\bm{x}}V_{\mathbb{\Theta}}({\bm{x}},s)\cdot\overline{\bm{W}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}({\bm{x}},s)}\;d{\bm{x}}ds

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}{\mathscr{S}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}V_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\textnormal{{x}})\cdot\overline{\bm{W}(\textnormal{{x}})}\;d\textnormal{{x}}\quad\textnormal{from \eqref{H2Dmod:eq:oblique_plancherel}}

\displaystyle=\big\langle{\mathscr{S}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}V_{\mathbb{\Theta}},\;\bm{W}\big\rangle_{[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})^{2}]^{\prime},\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})^{2}}.

(A.4)

The identity (
A.4
) being true for any
\bm{W}\in[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})]^{2}
implies that
\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}V={\mathscr{S}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}V_{\mathbb{\Theta}}
in the sense of distributions. But thanks to the bijectivity of
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
and to the fact that
\nabla_{\bm{x}}V_{\mathbb{\Theta}}\in L^{2}(0,1;[L^{2}(Q)]^{2})
, it follows that
{\mathscr{S}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}V_{\mathbb{\Theta}}\in[L^{2}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})]^{2}
. Therefore,
\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}V
also belongs to
[L^{2}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})]^{2}
, and one has

\displaystyle\operatorname{a.e.}\;\textnormal{{x}}\in\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}},\quad\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\bm{\nabla}V(\textnormal{{x}})={\mathscr{S}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}}\nabla_{\bm{x}}V_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\textnormal{{x}}).

(A.5)

Consequently,
V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
and (
3.17
) follows by applying the transform
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}
to (
A.5
).

A.3
Proof of Proposition
3.5
: density of smooth functions in
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}),H_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbf{div};\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})

Proof
(of Proposition
3.5
).

We prove the first item, since the proof of the second one is very similar. For
V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
, we construct a sequence of smooth functions that converges to
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V\in L^{2}(0,1;H^{1}(Q))
. For almost any
{\bm{x}}\in Q
, let
\zeta_{n}({\bm{x}},\cdot)
denote the
n
–th partial Fourier sum of
{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V({\bm{x}},\cdot)
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{a.e.}\;({\bm{x}},s)\in Q\times(0,1),\quad\zeta_{n}({\bm{x}},s):=\sum_{\ell=-n}^{n}\widehat{V}^{(\ell)}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}({\bm{x}})\,\mathrm{e}^{2\mathrm{i}\pi js},\quad\widehat{V}^{(\ell)}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}({\bm{x}}):=\int_{0}^{1}{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V({\bm{x}},t)\,\mathrm{e}^{-2\mathrm{i}\pi\ell t}\,dt.

It can be checked easily that
\zeta({\bm{x}},\cdot)\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}([0,1])
is
1
–periodic, and that
\zeta(\cdot,s)\in H^{1}(Q)
(as a
finite
linear combination of
\widehat{V}^{(j)}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}\in H^{1}(Q)
). Moreover, using dominated convergence theorem, one shows that
\zeta_{n}\to{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V
in
L^{2}(0,1;H^{1}(Q))
,
n\to+\infty
.

Now, consider a sequence of functions
\widehat{V}^{(\ell,m)}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\overline{Q})
converging to
\widehat{V}^{(\ell)}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}
in
H^{1}(Q)
, and define

\displaystyle\forall\;({\bm{x}},s)\in Q\times(0,1),\quad\zeta_{n,m}({\bm{x}},s):=\sum_{\ell=-n}^{n}\widehat{V}^{(\ell,m)}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}({\bm{x}})\,\mathrm{e}^{2\mathrm{i}\pi\ell s}.

For any
(m,n)\in\mathbb{N}^{2}
,
\zeta_{n,m}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}(\overline{Q\times(0,1)})
is
1
–periodic with respect to
s
. Moreover, from the above, we deduce by the triangle inequality that
\zeta_{n,n}\to{\mathscr{S}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\mskip 1.5mu}V
in
L^{2}(0,1;H^{1}(Q))
,
n\to+\infty
. As a consequence,
{\mathscr{S}^{-1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}}}\zeta_{n,n}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\overline{\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})\to V
in
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\mathbb{\Omega}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
,
n\to+\infty
.

Appendix B
Proof of Proposition
4.6
: properties of the Floquet-Bloch transform

We prove the properties of the Floquet-Bloch transform stated in Proposition
4.6
by adapting the ideas of the isotropic case
[
31
]
.

Proof
.

Item
(a)
, Step
1
– We first show that
{\mathcal{F}}
is bounded from
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))
, and that (
4.14
) holds. First, one computes directly that

\forall\;\zeta\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}),\quad\operatorname{a.e.}\;{k}\in(-\pi,\pi),\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathcal{F}}\zeta(\cdot,{k})\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\overline{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}),\\[12.0pt]
{\mathcal{F}}(\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,\bm{\nabla}\zeta)\mskip 1.5mu(\cdot,{k})=\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,{\mathcal{F}}\zeta(\cdot,{k}).\end{array}\right.

This implies from the Parseval-like formula (
4.11
) (with
\bm{U}=\bm{V}=\zeta,\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,\bm{\nabla}\zeta
) that
{\mathcal{F}}
is continuous from
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\overline{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}))
(which are equipped respectively with the
H^{1}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
–norm and the
L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;H^{1}_{\mathbb{\Theta}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))
–norm). By
density
(Proposition
3.5
),
{\mathcal{F}}
extends to a continuous map from
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))
, which satisfies (
4.14
).

Item
(a)
, Step
2
– It remains to show that
{\mathcal{F}^{-1}}
(see (
4.12
)) is bounded from
L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))
to
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
. To do so, it is sufficient to prove that

\displaystyle\forall\;\widehat{V}\in L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})),\quad\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,\bm{\nabla}{\mathcal{F}^{-1}}\widehat{V}={\mathcal{F}^{-1}}[\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\,\widehat{V}].

(B.1)

For isotropic Sobolev spaces (
{\mathbb{\Theta}}=\mathbb{I}_{3}
), the classical way to prove (
B.1
) is by using the jump rule, which involves traces on the faces
{z}_{1}\in\{0,1\}
. But since we have not defined these traces in this paper, we shall resort to the Green’s formula (
3.33
), obtained by density. Given
\bm{W}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
, using derivation in the sense of distributions with
{\mathcal{F}^{-1}}\widehat{V}\in L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
gives

\displaystyle\big\langle\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,\bm{\nabla}{\mathcal{F}^{-1}}\widehat{V},\bm{W}\big\rangle_{[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})^{2}]^{\prime},\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}{\mathcal{F}^{-1}}\widehat{V}(\textnormal{{x}})\,\overline{\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut\bm{\nabla}}{\mathbb{\Theta}}\bm{W}(\textnormal{{x}})}\;d\textnormal{{x}}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}\widehat{V}(\textnormal{{x}},{k})\,\overline{{\mathcal{F}}\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut\bm{\nabla}}{\mathbb{\Theta}}\bm{W}(\textnormal{{x}},{k})}\;d\textnormal{{x}}\,d{k}\quad\textnormal{from \eqref{H2Dmod:eq:partial_TFB_plancherel}}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}\widehat{V}(\textnormal{{x}},{k})\,\overline{\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})}\,{\mathbb{\Theta}}{\mathcal{F}}\bm{W}(\textnormal{{x}},{k})}\;d\textnormal{{x}}d{k},

(B.2)

where the last equality is obtained from direct computations. Since
\widehat{V}(\cdot,{k})\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})
and
\bm{W}
is smooth, we can apply Green’s formula (
3.33
) to (
B.2
), to deduce that

\displaystyle\big\langle\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,\bm{\nabla}{\mathcal{F}^{-1}}\widehat{V},\bm{W}\big\rangle_{[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})^{2}]^{\prime},\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\mskip 1.5mu\widehat{V}(\textnormal{{x}},{k})\,\overline{{\mathcal{F}}\bm{W}(\textnormal{{x}},{k})}\;d\textnormal{{x}}d{k}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}{\mathcal{F}^{-1}}[\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\widehat{V}](\textnormal{{x}})\,\overline{\bm{W}(\textnormal{{x}})}\;d\textnormal{{x}}\quad\textnormal{from \eqref{H2Dmod:eq:partial_TFB_plancherel}}

\displaystyle=\big\langle{\mathcal{F}^{-1}}[\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\widehat{V}],\bm{W}\big\rangle_{[\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})^{2}]^{\prime},\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})^{2}}.

The above implies (
B.1
) in the sense of distributions. But since
{\mathcal{F}^{-1}}[\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,(\bm{\nabla}+\mathrm{i}\mskip 1.5mu{k}\mskip 1.5mu\textnormal{{e}}_{1})\widehat{V}]\in L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
, we deduce that
\mskip 1.0mu\prescript{{\mathrm{t}\,\mkern-3.0mu}}{}{\mathstrut{\mathbb{\Theta}}}\,\bm{\nabla}{\mathcal{F}^{-1}}\widehat{V}\in L^{2}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
, so that (
B.1
) holds.

Item
(b)
– The proof is very similar to the one of Item
(a)
, and therefore is omitted.

Item
(c)
– For the sake of clarity in this part, we highlight the distinction between the
volume
Floquet-Bloch transform
{\mathcal{F}}_{v}
for functions in
\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
and the
\emph{surface}
Floquet-Bloch transform
{\mathcal{F}}_{s}
for functions on
\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
. In addition, let
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
(
resp.

\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}
) denote the trace operator on
\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}
(
resp.

\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}
). We begin by proving that the Floquet-Bloch transform commutes with the trace operator in the following sense:

\displaystyle\forall\;V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}),\quad{\mathcal{F}}_{s}\,(\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}V)=\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}\,({\mathcal{F}}_{v}V).

(B.3)

Note that (
B.3
) is straightforward for smooth functions
V\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\overline{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})
. It extends to
V\in H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
using
(i)
the density of
\smash{\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\overline{\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}})}
in
\smash{H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})}
(Proposition
3.5
),
(ii)
the continuity of
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
and
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}
(Proposition
3.8
),
(iii)
the continuity of
{\mathcal{F}}_{v}
from
H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))
(proved in Item
(a)
), and finally
(iv)
the continuity of
{\mathcal{F}}_{s}
from
L^{2}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;L^{2}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))
.

Now we can show Item
(c)
, namely that the surface Floquet-Bloch transform
{\mathcal{F}}_{s}
is an isomorphism from
H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})
to
L^{2}(-\pi,\pi;H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))
. Since
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}
and
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}
are surjective, there exist two bounded operators
\mathcal{R}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}\in\mathscr{L}(\smash{H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}})},H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}))
and
\mathcal{R}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}\in\mathscr{L}(\smash{H^{1/2}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Sigma_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}})},H^{1}_{{\mathbb{\Theta}}\mskip 1.5mu{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}(\Omega_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}))
such that
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\,\mathcal{R}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}=I
and
\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}\,\mathcal{R}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}=I
. Thus (
B.3
) leads to

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{s}=\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}}\,{\mathcal{F}}_{v}\,\mathcal{R}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad[{\mathcal{F}}_{s}]^{-1}=\gamma_{0,{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle\#$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle\#$}}}}\,[{\mathcal{F}}_{v}]^{-1}\,\mathcal{R}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.875}{$\displaystyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\textstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}{\scalebox{0.875}{$\scriptscriptstyle{\#}^{2}$}}}.

Using Item
(a)
then allows to conclude.
